# QUEUE.md — PitchNotes-iOS task board

> The ONLY task board (AGENTS.md §2). Every agent: edit ONLY your own section,
> append to LOG (newest at TOP). Queue edits are tiny standalone commits
> (`queue: <claim/done/build> <task-id>`) — never bundled with code, pushed immediately.

Task row format (one line per task, keep it short):

```
- [P-014] DOING | swiftf0 ONNX spike on macOS runner | branch:paul | dep:— | build:<run-url>@<sha> | ac:spike asserts pitch+confidence on 3 fixtures | note:<handoff/next-step>
```

- IDs: `P-###` Paul, `J-###` Jess, `L-###` Leto, `X-###` cross-cutting (Leto assigns owner).
- Status lifecycle: `TODO → DOING → REVIEW → DONE`, plus `BLOCKED(reason)`;
  `BUILD_PENDING` is a *flag on the row*, never a reason to stop working.
- `ac:` = acceptance criteria. A task without `ac:` is not claimable; write one first.
- `note:` = handoff. EVERY finished task ends with a note telling the next reader
  exactly what exists, where, and what's next.
- Max one DOING coding task per agent; unlimited pending CI builds (tracked on the
  row via `build:`, not in your head).

---

So the 1105 gapless delay = 576 (encoder side) + 529 (decoder side) and the decoders apply DIFFERENT parts of it: ffmpeg's raw decode applies all 1105 (aligns at 1105); **CoreAudio applies the 529-sample decoder-side delay but not the LAME gapless trim → aligns at 1105 − 529 = 576** (the runner's measured value). The @5258632 run is the judge (in flight at the handoff). **THEN @5258632 run 45094828320 RED AGAIN — ONE error left:** `ALACStemWriter.swift:111: error: cannot convert value of type 'AVAudioPCMBuffer' to expected argument type 'CMSampleBuffer'` — `AVAssetWriterInput.append` on this SDK takes a **CMSampleBuffer**, not an AVAudioPCMBuffer (I wrote the AVAudioPCMBuffer overload from memory; it does not exist). TWO candidate fixes (pick one; CI is the judge, and Linux can verify NEITHER — `swiftc -parse` only): **(A) preferred — drop AVAssetWriter entirely and use `AVAudioFile(forWriting:settings:commonFormat:interleaved:)` + `write(from:)`** (~15 lines: settings `{AVFormatIDKey: kAudioFormatAppleLossless, AVSampleRateKey: 44100, AVNumberOfChannelsKey: channels, AVEncoderBitDepthHintKey: <bit depth>}`, feed the source's chunks; note the appended buffer must be in the OUTPUT file's processingFormat — create it with the same commonFormat as the buffer you feed). **(B) keep AVAssetWriter and wrap each chunk into a CMSampleBuffer** (`CMAudioFormatDescription` from the source `AVAudioFormat` + `CMSampleBufferCreate` with an `AudioBufferList` built from the buffer's channel planes — the deinterleaved source mixes naturally with it). Either way keep the existing assertions (lossless round-trip test asserts max|Δ| < 1.5/32767; the engine tests assert the bundle validates).** **REMAINING:** (1) CI verdict for the Apple-side code at @5258632 (X-204 step); (2) "sizes logged (~5–6 MB/min/stem)" — the writer does not log sizes yet; (3) the cache index is engine-side only — if Leto meant D-4's library index, that is Jess's J-202 and needs a ruling. **ALAC WRITER REWRITTEN ON `AVAudioFile` (fix A, @9e28910 pushed; CI run 45140277183 in flight):** the `AVAssetWriterInput.append(CMSampleBuffer)` route is GONE — the writer now uses `AVAudioFile(forWriting:settings:commonFormat:.pcmFormatFloat32,interleaved:false)` (the ALAC encoder's only accepted layout) and streams 8 192-frame chunks into it (per-plane float32 copy; both sides deinterleaved), finalising the `.m4a` by releasing the file inside a nested scope BEFORE the size is measured and before the orchestrator validates/reads it (the old AVAssetWriter path finalised at `finishWriting`, the new one at dealloc — both happen before the call returns). **P-204's "sizes logged" ac leg is NOW DONE:** `encodeStemWithInfo` returns `EncodedStemInfo{part, channels, bytes, durationSeconds, megabytesPerMinute}` (logged via os.Logger, subsystem com.thesagezm.pitchnotes / category import) and the new macOS test `testALACStemWriterReportsEncodedSize` asserts the report matches the file on disk (bytes>0, duration 1.0 s, finite MB/min). NOTE on the §8 figure: 5–6 MB/min is for REAL music — a synthetic sine compresses far below it, so the test asserts the SIZE REPORT, not the §8 ratio (the ratio is checked against a real import at P-603). **RUNTIME DEFECTS FIXED @b7aecc2 (run 45153726873 IN FLIGHT) — CI run 45143126945@afd1080 was RED on TWO runtime defects, NOT on compile (the module and the tests compile and run):** **(A) the ALAC encode failed at runtime in all three ALAC tests** with ONE opaque error ("The operation couldn't be completed. (Foundation._GenericObjCError error 0.)") — the outer catch swallowed WHICH call failed, so the message said nothing. FIX: the writer now runs a STRATEGY LADDER and reports PER-STEP diagnostics (open / read / copy / write) with the NSError's domain, code and userInfo; every attempt's failure is collected and the aggregate error names them all, so ONE CI round trip is decisive. The ladder covers the two causes that could not be ruled out from Linux (the ALAC encoder is Apple-only): (a) the `AVEncoderBitDepthHintKey` hint CONTRADICTING the float32 client format (hint and no-hint variants), and (b) the `read(into:frameCount:)` overload refusing a SHORT final read (a 44 100-frame source ends on a partial 3 140-frame chunk — the older `read(into:)` overload, the one the decoder uses, is the alternative); plus the int16 client layout. Each attempt opens a FRESH source file (a failed attempt leaves the read position mid-file). The winning strategy is logged. **(B) the two engine tests threw a MASKING error** — "Error Domain=NSCocoaErrorDomain Code=4 "“.work” couldn’t be removed." (NSFilePath=<test-temp>/library/.work, underlying POSIX ENOENT) — which REPLACED the real ALAC failure. The only ".work" path in the tree is the engine's work dir (libraryRoot/.work, created ONLY by the standalone `importAudio` flow — the import path never creates it), and removing a path that was never created throws. FIX (both directions): the engine's deinit removes the work dir ONLY if it EXISTS and swallows+logs any failure (never throws); `ImportOrchestrator` gained `removeIfPresent` (idempotent, never throws — the failure is RETURNED as a message) and `preserving` (the ORIGINAL error's type and message survive; a cleanup note is APPENDED, never substituted), so **a failed import surfaces the REAL error**. 4 new Linux tests cover the cleanup helpers (EngineCore suite). **REMAINING:** (1) the CI verdict for @b7aecc2 (X-204 step — the judge; if the ALAC write still fails, the aggregate error now names the exact step and cause); (2) the cache index is engine-side only — if Leto meant D-4's library index, that is Jess's J-202 and needs a ruling. **DONE (queue): run 45156291152@1935a0f GREEN 8/8 — the ALAC writer WORKS at runtime (all three ALAC tests pass, incl. the LOSSLESS round-trip: max|Δ| < 1.5/32767), the two engine tests pass with the REAL error surfacing (no more '.work' masking), the size report matches the file on disk, the bundle validates V1–V15, and EngineCore is 191/191 (187 + the 4 new cleanup tests). Every ac leg is met: ALAC decodes natively ✓, the bundle validates against CONTRACTS ✓, sizes are logged/reported ✓ (§8's 5–6 MB/min ratio is checked against a REAL import at P-603, not against a synthetic sine). STILL OPEN (not mine): the engine-side cache index is NOT D-4's library index (that is Jess's J-202) — a ruling is needed only if the app needs a different shape. (4) the engine-failure, the vendor-cancellation and the task-cancellation tests all assert the sinks are ABORTED and NO partial stem file survives (`PCM16StreamWriter.abort()` removes the file). (5) the progress test asserts monotonicity inside the separating band [0.05, 0.95]. (6) the Apple path is rewritten onto `runStreaming` (`Engine/Separation/DemucsEngine.swift`; macOS typecheck green locally + the macOS build-ipa green on the runner). (b) the streaming writer patched the RIFF size and the data size as ONE contiguous block at offset 4 — it overwrote the "WAVE"/"fmt " identifiers (the staged stems would have been unreadable); the two sizes are now patched separately (offset 4 and offset 40) and the test asserts the streamed file is byte-identical to `PCMWavWriter`'s; (c) a test expectation was wrong (the driver does NOT emit its own 1.0 when the caller owns completion — the band top 0.95 is correct). **HONEST LIMITS:** the DECODE stage still holds the decoded song once (the decoder's own buffer — a separate term, not part of separation); the C++ benchmark mirror still accumulates in RAM (it is a harness, not the shipping path); **no A14 device number is claimed.** NEXT: nothing in my lane is blocked — the P-202 D-25 ruling is LETO's (the landing site is named on the P-202 row). | note:gate-closed 2026-11-24 (Leto gate review): the streaming stem writer is MERGED to main @be0c700 (paul->main #13) — run 45186103363@d42e120 ALL 8 GREEN, EngineCore 204/204; absence = "no corrections"; the calibration lives beside it as `corrections/song.json`); the undo order is the SESSION order (an in-memory `undoStack: [Part]` of the parts in the order the user appended to them; undo pops the last part and removes its LAST operation) with a STATED DETERMINISTIC FALLBACK after a relaunch (parts in C-1 order, each part's operations in order) — and **THE UI SAYS SO IN PLAIN WORDS** ("Undo removes the last correction in contract part order (T1, T2, …), because these corrections were loaded from the file — there is no session order to follow."); the corrections list is GROUPED BY PART (C-1 order, operations in order within a part) with a label that says exactly that; a REASSIGN's op lands in the overlay of `op.fromPart` (the part whose ORIGINAL array holds the note) — so a reassign to another part lands in the SOURCE part's file, not the destination's; the SPLIT UI is just forwarded (`PianoRollView` already emits the contract-shaped `CorrectionOperation`); the calibration (renames + ranges) is the user's DISPLAY PREFERENCE, not a note correction, so "clear all corrections" does NOT destroy it (it has its own "Reset names & ranges" button, and the confirmation dialog says the names and ranges are kept); **A FILE WE COULD NOT READ IS NEVER DELETED BY A SAVE** (only successfully-loaded parts may be removed by a save) — the user's bytes are never destroyed by a save that did not touch that part, and the quarantine copy stays; the V8 REPAIR IS TRIMMED AND REPORTED (never silently applied); the DRAG-DROP AC IS MET AS AN EXPLICIT, VOICEOVER-FRIENDLY "Move to another part" ACTION in the note sheet (one tap: note sheet → part) — this roll's vertical axis is PITCH, so there is no lane to drop onto, and I do not pretend one exists; a reassign without `midi` OMITS the `midi` key), the round-trip, unknown-`op` refusal, split-≠2 refusal (decode AND encode), resolution by ORIGINAL index, confidence preserved on a corrected note, corrected notes MARKED (`correctedNotes` + `origins`), split semantics + confidence override, split-not-inside ⇒ SKIPPED+REPORTED, noteIndex-out-of-range ⇒ SKIPPED+REPORTED, order semantics, raw tracks NEVER mutated, no-overlay ⇒ raw tracks exactly, clearing restores the engine output exactly, the V8 repair trimmed + REPORTED, the range-override suite (preset/nudge/clamp/custom/24…96/ordered/no-preset/counted-not-rejected + X-216's exact stored shape), the absence rule; I STORE the C-6.1 table's own `presetName` ("G3–E5") and the decoder DERIVES THE BOUNDS FROM THE CONTRACT TABLE BY PART (the dictionary key), so the stored preset name is purely informational and the bounds are never taken from it — a file saying "TENOR_1" also decodes correctly (same bounds, that name displayed); LETO MUST SAY whether the stored `preset` string is the C-6.1 range label (what I do) or a new stable preset identifier like `TENOR_1` (which would need its own table) — the two are NOT interchangeable. (ii) THE DELETE-THEN-REASSIGN ORDER — under the semantics the handoff itself blessed ("an op targets the ORIGINAL note and acts on whatever that note has BECOME"), delete-FIRST-then-reassign leaves nothing for the reassign to move: the note is GONE and the reassign is SKIPPED+REPORTED (the note is NOT in the new part); that is what the code does and what my test pins (`testTheReverseOrderDeletesFirstAndTheReassignIsReported`) — if Leto rules the other way the fix is ONE TEST + ONE BRANCH in `CorrectionResolver.apply`. **IF LETO NAMES A DIFFERENT HOME FOR THE CALIBRATION FILE, ONLY `SongCalibrationOverlay` + `CorrectionStore`'s two path helpers change** — everything else is already per-part.

  **(1) THE RANGE-OVERRIDE + PART-RENAME HAVE NO HOME IN C-11.** C-11 covers NOTE OPERATIONS only and stores them one file per corrected part in `corrections/<PART>.json`. But J-302's AC also requires a **per-song RANGE OVERRIDE** (C-6.1's own comment asks for it: "per-song custom ranges arrive with the M3 calibration task (J-302) and extend this table additively") and a **part RENAME**. Neither is a note operation, so neither can be expressed as a C-11 operation against a `noteIndex`. **PROPOSAL — a sibling file `corrections/song.json` inside the same bundle directory** (it is the same overlay concept, same lifetime, same "absence = no overrides" rule, and it keeps EVERYTHING the user edited inside ONE reversible place):**
  ```json
  {
    "contractVersion": "v1.1",
    "engineVersion": "corrections-0.1.0",
    "partRenames": { "T1": "Tenor 1", "BARITENOR": "Bari" },
    "rangeOverrides": {
      "T1": { "preset": "TENOR_1", "nudgeSemitones": 2 },
      "BASS": { "custom": { "minMidi": 40, "maxMidi": 60 } }
    }
  }
  ```
  **THE TWO RANGE SHAPES, EXACTLY:** `{preset, nudgeSemitones}` — `preset` is a C-6.1 preset name, `nudgeSemitones` is an integer in **−2…+2** (C-6.1's own ±2 semitone calibration bound; my current implementation clamps to it) — OR `{custom: {minMidi, maxMidi}}` — both ints, `minMidi <= maxMidi`, both clamped into **V9's 24…96**. The two shapes are mutually exclusive within one part's entry. `partRenames` maps a C-1 `Part` rawValue to a display string (the C-1 raw values NEVER change — a rename is a DISPLAY layer only). **ABSENCE RULE (binding, same as C-10):** absent file ⇒ NO overrides and NO renames ⇒ the consumer behaves EXACTLY as it does today; an empty `partRenames`/`rangeOverrides` ⇒ same. **THE CONTRACT TABLE (C-6.1) IS NEVER MUTATED — the override EXTENDS it additively.** **IF YOU PREFER A DIFFERENT HOME** (e.g. `calibration/<PART>.json`, or a `calibration` array inside `song.json`), say so and I will implement YOUR shape — but the file must (a) live inside the bundle directory (C-11's rule: the overlay lives beside the bundle's notes), (b) be OPTIONAL with the same absence rule, and (c) NOT touch `notes/<PART>.json` (C-11 rule 1) or `song.json`'s frozen v1.0 fields (C-2.1 — I will not write to the manifest at all).
  **(2) THE `split` SEMANTICS DIFFER FROM MY DRAFT — I AM ALIGNING TO C-11, CONFIRMING THE INTERPRETATION.** C-11's `split` is `{op:"split", fromPart, noteIndex, splitAt_s, toParts:["T1","T2"]}` — the note becomes **TWO notes in the NAMED parts** (each keeping the original note's confidence unless the operation overrides it). My session-3 draft split the note **in place** (same part, two halves) — that is NOT what C-11 says, so I am dropping my draft's semantics and implementing C-11's: `toParts` MUST name exactly **two** C-1 parts; the FIRST half goes to `toParts[0]`, the second to `toParts[1]`; `splitAt_s` MUST be strictly inside the note (`note.start_s < splitAt_s < note.end_s`) — a split outside the note is a CONTRACT VIOLATION and is REPORTED (never silently clamped); the operation MAY override the confidence of the halves (the contract says "unless the operation overrides it" — I propose an OPTIONAL `confidence` key on the split op). **PLEASE CONFIRM: (a) that `toParts` is ordered first-half/second-half, and (b) that an optional `confidence` override on the split op is acceptable — I will implement it as stated only after you rule.**
  **(3) THE MusicXML DOCUMENT TYPE / UTI FOR J-304 (`project.yml` is YOUR lane).** J-304 must let the user open a `.musicxml` / `.mxl` hymn score. **THE EXACT `Info.plist` ENTRIES (add to the `PitchNotesIOS` target's `info.properties` in project.yml — the app target already sets `INFOPLIST_KEY_*` style keys, so these go in the same block):**
  ```yaml

  UTExportedTypeDeclarations:
    - UTTypeIdentifier: com.recordare.musicxml
      UTTypeDescription: MusicXML Score
      UTTypeConformsTo:
        - public.xml
      UTTypeTagSpecification:
        public.filename-extension:
          - musicxml
          - xml
        public.mime-type:
          - application/vnd.recordare.musicxml+xml
    - UTTypeIdentifier: com.recordare.musicxml.compressed
      UTTypeDescription: Compressed MusicXML Score
      UTTypeConformsTo:
        - public.zip-archive
      UTTypeTagSpecification:
        public.filename-extension:

  ```
  **WHY THE SECOND UTI:** `.mxl` is a ZIP container, not XML — `UTTypeConformsTo: public.zip-archive` is what makes Files hand us a compressed score. **WHY I AM NOT SURE THIS IS ENOUGH:** if the app also needs to OPEN a score by URL from inside the app (the J-304 flow: "import MusicXML" from the note-track screen), the same two UTIs + `UISupportsDocumentBrowser`-style handling may not be required — the in-app `fileImporter` can filter by extension directly. **I ONLY NEED THE UTI SO THE SYSTEM CAN HAND THE APP A SCORE; if you rule that the in-app picker is sufficient for the M3 AC, say so and I will build against the picker only and drop this X-task's plist half.** No contract change, no new dependency, zero runtime network.

EXACT SPEC BELOW — I AM NOT FORKING LANE-LOCAL CONTRACT TYPES, PER AGENTS §7):**
  **THE SITUATION (verified, not assumed):** CONTRACTS v1.1 C-10.0 (L-301, D-44) is RATIFIED and defines a `notes/<PART>.json` at v1.1 with optional per-note and per-file metadata. `origin/paul @ e38896b` has **NO `assignmentBand`, NO `partPresence`, NO `assignmentSummary`** in `EngineCore`'s `Note`/`NoteTrack` — `EngineCore.NoteTrack` still has exactly `contractVersion`, `part`, `notes`. **AGENTS §7 forbids lane-local copies of contract types**, so I did NOT fork a `NoteTrack` in PitchLogic; instead my roll derives D-41's band/presence/unison locally from `confidence` — which is exactly C-10's **ABSENCE RULE** behaviour, so the app is CORRECT today. But it means the app cannot PREFER the file's own metadata, and C-10 says it MUST prefer it when present.
  **WHAT I NEED FROM YOU — C-10.0's JSON KEYS VERBATIM, ADDED TO `EngineCore.Note` / `EngineCore.NoteTrack` AS OPTIONAL FIELDS:**
  ```json
  { "contractVersion": "v1.1", "part": "BASS", "engineVersion": "basicpitch-0.3.0",
    "notes": [ { "start_s": 1.232, "end_s": 2.500, "midi": 48, "cents_ref": 12,
                 "confidence": 0.91, "unison": false, "salience": 0.88,
                 "assignmentBand": "CONFIRMED" } ],
    "partPresence": [ { "phraseIndex": 0, "start_s": 0.0, "end_s": 4.0,
                        "present": true, "meanConfidence": 0.87, "noteCount": 6 } ],
    "assignmentSummary": { "confirmed": 5, "provisional": 1, "unassigned": 0 } }
  ```
  **THE EXACT SHAPE, FIELD BY FIELD:**
  - **per note, `EngineCore.Note`:** `unison: Bool?` (absent ⇒ treat as `false`); `salience: Double?` (absent ⇒ the consumer behaves EXACTLY as today — no salience filtering/weighting/rendering/grading change; when present it is a DISPLAY/ANALYSIS hint and MUST NOT change which notes are graded nor hide a note on its own — C-12); `assignmentBand: String?` (absent ⇒ the consumer DERIVES the band from `confidence` at D-41's thresholds; present ⇒ exactly one of `"CONFIRMED"` (c ≥ 0.80) / `"PROVISIONAL"` (0.50 ≤ c < 0.80) / `"UNASSIGNED"` (c < 0.50), raw values stable forever). `confidence` is **NOT redefined** — C-2.3's field, unchanged; it is simply REQUIRED per note when the FILE's `contractVersion == "v1.1"` (V16).
  - **per file, `EngineCore.NoteTrack`:** `engineVersion: String?` (ADDITIONAL to the manifest's `engineVersion`, never replacing it — C-10.2; absent ⇒ "unknown", never refuse the file); `partPresence: [PartPresence]?` where `PartPresence = { phraseIndex: Int, start_s: Double, end_s: Double, present: Bool, meanConfidence: Double, noteCount: Int }` (absent ⇒ the consumer MUST NOT dim or hide anything; V17: ordered by `phraseIndex` ascending, `start_s < end_s`, `meanConfidence` in [0,1], `noteCount` ≥ 0, `present == (noteCount >= 1 && meanConfidence >= 0.60)`, and `present == false` is NOT an error); `assignmentSummary: AssignmentSummary?` where `AssignmentSummary = { confirmed: Int, provisional: Int, unassigned: Int }` (absent ⇒ the consumer MAY compute the counts; it MUST NOT fail).
  **THE ABSENCE RULE IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IT IS BINDING:** *every field above is OPTIONAL, and a consumer MUST behave exactly as it did before v1.1 when the field is absent.* Absent metadata is never a validation error, never a reason to reject a bundle, never a reason to fail an import. **A v0.0/v1.0 note file stays valid forever and MUST still validate under V7–V10 only** (V16 does not apply to it). **A consumer written before v1.1 MUST work unchanged on a file that carries v1.1 fields.**
  **V18 (also yours, same file):** if `assignmentBand` is present on a note, it MUST agree with `confidence` under D-41's thresholds. **A mismatch is a CONTRACT VIOLATION, not something to silently repair** — the engine fixes it, the consumer reports it.
  **WHY I AM NOT DOING IT MYSELF:** `Engine/**` is your lane and §7 says no lane-local copies of contract types — a second `NoteTrack` in PitchLogic would mean two semantics for one contract (exactly the class of bug the AGENTS rail exists to prevent). **WHEN IT LANDS, MY SWAP IS ONE PLACE:** `PianoRollModel` reads the file's `unison`/`salience`/`assignmentBand`/`partPresence`/`assignmentSummary`/per-track `engineVersion` and falls back to the local D-41 derivation when they are absent — **the UI does not change** (the roll already consumes `[Part: NoteTrack]`, so P-305's mock→real swap stays zero-UI-change).
  **NO CONTRACT CHANGE (C-10 IS ALREADY RATIFIED), NO NEW DEPENDENCY, NO APP-CHANGE.** If you prefer a different Swift type layout (a nested `NoteTrack.PartPresence` struct, an enum for `assignmentBand`, a separate `AssignmentBand` type), that is entirely yours — **I only need the JSON to be C-10.0's keys, verbatim, and optional.** If you cannot take it in this milestone, say so in the LOG and I will keep the local D-41 derivation and record the swap as outstanding — but please do not merge a HALF version: a file that carries `assignmentBand` and a consumer that ignores it is a silent wrongness, which is exactly what C-10's absence rule is designed to prevent.   **(i) THERE IS NO TIME-SHIFT OP — a manual nudge of a SCORE note has NO persisted home.** C-11's op set is EXACTLY `"reassign"` / `"delete"` / `"split"` and its ops address the ENGINE's original `notes[]` by `noteIndex`; the SCORE is a different artifact entirely (C-13's `scoreNotes`, with its own `start_s`/`end_s`/`measure`/`part_id`), so a score-note time offset cannot be expressed as a C-11 operation against an engine note. **THE STATE TODAY IS HONEST AND VISIBLE:** the nudge works IN-SESSION (an in-memory, per-score-index offset, shown in the UI, undoable, and it moves the derived score-informed track) and the screen SAYS IN PLAIN WORDS that it is NOT saved. **PROPOSAL — LETO PICKS THE HOME (both are additive; neither changes C-11's existing ops):**

    (`scoreIndex` = the index in the C-13.1 `scoreNotes` array; `offset_s` in seconds, additive, may be negative. The file is OPTIONAL; absent ⇒ no nudges.)
  - **(B) A NEW OP IN C-11** — e.g. `{"op":"nudge","fromPart":"…","noteIndex":n,"offset_s":x}` — **WHICH I DO NOT RECOMMEND:** C-11's ops address the engine's notes, and a score-note index is a different coordinate space; mixing them in one file would make the two index spaces ambiguous.
  **(MY PREFERENCE IS (A), because it keeps the two artifacts (the engine's notes and the score's notes) in two files with two coordinate spaces, and it matches the pattern X-216 already proposes for the calibration (`corrections/song.json` beside the part files).)**
  **(ii) THE SCORE-PART → C-1-PART MAPPING HAS NO HOME IN THE CONTRACT JSON.** C-13.1 says the `part_id` → C-1-part mapping "is the alignment's business", but C-13.2's alignment JSON has NO field for it (`{confidence, used, barMap, method}`) ⇒ **a consumer that reads ONLY the contract JSON cannot rebuild a per-part score track** — it would have to guess, and guessing a part is exactly what C-13/D-41 forbid. **TODAY I CARRY IT OUTSIDE THE JSON** (`ScoreAlignmentResult.partMapping`, a lane-local result type, NOT written to disk), which means the mapping is lost when the alignment is persisted. **PROPOSAL — ADD ONE FIELD TO C-13.2's alignment object, additively:**
  ```json
  { "contractVersion": "v1.1",
    "alignment": { "confidence": 0.82, "used": true, "barMap": [ … ],
                   "method": "DTW",
                   "partMapping": { "P1": "T1", "P2": "T2", "P5": "BASS" } } }
  ```
  (`partMapping` = score `part_id` → C-1 part rawValue; OPTIONAL, absent ⇒ no score note is put into any part — the same honest "unmapped" state I already report in the UI. The VALUES are C-1 `Part` rawValues, which are stable forever per §C-1.) **ASKING: does the contract carry it in the alignment object (my proposal), or somewhere else (e.g. a sibling field in the score-note-list file)?**
  **(iii) CONFIRM THE READING OF C-14's "it improved note agreement" — I READ IT STRICTLY, AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS WORTH A RULING.** My implementation: the BASELINE is the RAW score's agreement with the detected notes, and the alignment must make it **STRICTLY BETTER** (`after > before`; the agreement metric is a symmetric Dice-style ratio: same MIDI + overlapping intervals within 0.25 s). **THE CONSEQUENCE: a score that already matches the recording exactly is NOT used** (the alignment improved nothing ⇒ the heuristic track stays the reference), and an alignment that makes things worse is not used. **THE ALTERNATIVE READING IS "NOT WORSE"** (`after >= before`), under which a perfectly-matching score WOULD be used. **MY TWO NUMBERS SO LETO CAN SEE THE DIFF:** the DEMO fixture (54 score notes) gives before = 0.824 and after = 1.000 (a real difference, not a staged one). **I NEED THE RULING BECAUSE IT CHANGES WHICH TRACK IS GRADED** — and the UI text ("the alignment improved the match") is written to the strict reading. **IF LETO RULES "NOT WORSE", THE CHANGE IS ONE COMPARISON (`>` → `>=`) IN `ScoreReview.agreementImproved` PLUS ONE TEST; the strict reading is what my tests pin today.**
  **WHAT I DID NOT DO (all deliberate):** I did NOT fork a contract type in PitchLogic (AGENTS §7 — the C-13 shapes I consume are lane-local READERS of the contract's JSON, not a second definition of it); I did NOT write a MusicXML parser (D-40 — ONE parser, Paul's, in the portable core); I did NOT persist a nudge under an invented op; I did NOT guess a part mapping; and I did NOT treat the agreement as "improved" when it did not improve. **THE CODE THAT WOULD MOVE IF LETO RULES:** (i) `ScoreReview.nudges` + one new store method + the screen's save path (the nudge is already IN the UI and the review); (ii) `ScoreAlignmentResult.partMapping` would move INTO the C-13.2 JSON and the decoder would read it; (iii) one comparison + one test.

(d) `AVAudioFile.read(into:frameCount:)` returns VOID in this SDK, so `frames = try inputFile.read(...)` cannot compile — the frame count is `buffer.frameLength` AFTER the read: `try inputFile.read(into: buffer, frameCount: chunkFrames)` then `frames = buffer.frameLength`.** Neither changes the engine's surface, so my binding does not wait on them. **AND ONE MORE, DEEPER THAN ALL FOUR (found when the probe got past them): `AVAssetWriterInput.append(_:)` takes a `CMSampleBuffer`, NOT an `AVAudioPCMBuffer` — so `input.append(buffer)` cannot compile either (`ALACStemWriter.swift:110:34: error: cannot convert value of type 'AVAudioPCMBuffer' to expected argument type 'CMSampleBuffer'`). That is not a one-liner: the writer has to turn the PCM buffer into a CMSampleBuffer (e.g. `CMAudioSampleBufferCreateReadyWithPacketDescriptions` over the buffer's `audioBufferList`, or `CMSampleBufferCreate`), and the sample description has to come from the input format. It is a WARNING today, but it is an ERROR under the Swift 6 language mode, and it is a real data-race shape (an `NSLock` held across a suspension point is a bug, not just a lint). The scoped form (`lock.withLock { publishedBundleID = outcome.bundleID }`) fixes both.**
